# CF Tracking — Field-Level Detail (part 19 of 41)

Companion to [PracticeForceOneCFTracking](PracticeForceOneCFTracking.html). Domains in this part: **Gastroenterology**, **Oncology**, **Neurology**.

Every field of every published CF definition in these domains, exactly as the runtime renders it (read live from `/api/form-configurations`). `bind` is the write target when the definition overrides the field id.

| Acute UGIB: Risk Stratification, Resuscitation, and Endoscopic Therapy | UGIB Diagnosis, Risk Stratification, and Initial Resuscitation | `ugib_scores` | Glasgow-Blatchford Score, Rockall Score, and UGIB Risk Stratification | `select` |  |  |  |
| Acute UGIB: Risk Stratification, Resuscitation, and Endoscopic Therapy | UGIB Diagnosis, Risk Stratification, and Initial Resuscitation | `ugib_resuscitation` | UGIB Resuscitation, Transfusion Strategy, and Pre-Endoscopy Management — RESUSCITATION: IV ACCESS: 2 large-bore IVs (16G or larger); aggressive resuscitation initially; BLOOD TRANSFUSION: RESTRICTIVE STRATEGY (TRICC trial analogy; VILLANUEVA Lancet 2013 UGIB trial: restrictive Hgb threshold below 7 g/dL [transfuse to Hgb 7-9] vs liberal below 9 [transfuse to 9-11]: RESTRICTIVE SUPERIOR; 6-week mortality 5% vs 9%; P=0.02; rebleed 10% vs 16%; restrictive reduces portal pressure + reduces esophageal variceal rebleed); EXCEPTION: variceal UGIB + massive hemorrhage: more liberal transfusion acceptable; ACTIVELY BLEEDING: transfuse regardless of Hgb; TARGET Hgb ABOVE 7 (above 8 if CAD or hemodynamic instability); FFP + PLATELETS: coagulopathy (INR above 1.5; platelets below 50k: transfuse before endoscopy); MASSIVE TRANSFUSION PROTOCOL (MTP) if exsanguinating; GOAL: hemostasis not euvolemia (over-resuscitation = dilutional coagulopathy + increased portal pressure + variceal rebleed); CIRRHOSIS SPECIAL CONSIDERATIONS: coagulopathy in cirrhosis is NOT the same as anticoagulant coagulopathy (cirrhosis = rebalanced hemostasis: low pro- AND anticoagulant factors; INR misleads; TEG/ROTEM better; avoid empirical FFP for elevated INR in cirrhosis without active coagulopathy); ANTIBIOTICS IN CIRRHOSIS: IV ceftriaxone 1 g/day x7 days (NORFLOXACIN 400 mg twice daily: same efficacy but less IV requirement; ceftriaxone preferred in advanced cirrhosis/ascites; reduces bacterial infections + bacteremia + improves survival; START immediately with suspected variceal UGIB); HEPATIC ENCEPHALOPATHY PREVENTION: lactulose NG or PO; rifaximin; reduce protein absorption with bleeding; PROTON PUMP INHIBITOR: IV PPI BEFORE ENDOSCOPY (HIGH-DOSE: omeprazole 80 mg IV bolus then 8 mg/h infusion OR pantoprazole equivalent; reduces stigmata of hemorrhage at endoscopy; reduces endoscopic interventions; ENDOSCOPY TIMING: non-variceal: within 24h for hemodynamically stable; URGENT within 12h if: continued active bleeding; hemodynamic instability; suspected variceal bleeding; ELECTIVE within 24h: GBS score above 0 stable; TIMING variceal UGIB: within 12h of presentation; OCTREOTIDE START PRE-ENDOSCOPY (variceal): somatostatin analog; 50 mcg IV bolus then 50 mcg/h infusion x5 days; reduces portal pressure; reduces variceal bleeding rate; start BEFORE endoscopy in suspected variceal UGIB; TERLIPRESSIN (ADH analog; 2 mg IV Q4h x 5 days: not available in US; superior to octreotide in some trials; European preferred); ERYTHROMYCIN 250 mg IV 30-120 min before endoscopy: gastric motility agent (increases gastric emptying of clots; improves visualization; reduces need for second endoscopy; PROKINETIC); metoclopramide alternative; DO NOT GIVE erythromycin if QTc prolonged; ANTICOAGULANT REVERSAL: warfarin + INR above 2.5: 4-factor PCC (prothrombin complex concentrate: Kcentra 25-50 IU/kg; faster than FFP; better volume; FDA 2013) + vitamin K 10 mg IV; DABIGATRAN: idarucizumab (Praxbind); FACTOR Xa INHIBITORS: andexanet alfa (AndexXa; FDA 2018); ciraparantag (pending); ANTIPLATELET REVERSAL: platelet transfusion if below 50k or antiplatelet + significant hemorrhage (DDDAVP for mild antiplatelet effect) | `text` |  |  |  |
| Acute UGIB: Risk Stratification, Resuscitation, and Endoscopic Therapy | Endoscopic Hemostasis, Variceal Management, and Post-Procedure Care | `ugib_endoscopic` | Endoscopic Hemostasis Techniques for Non-Variceal UGIB | `select` |  |  |  |
| Acute UGIB: Risk Stratification, Resuscitation, and Endoscopic Therapy | Endoscopic Hemostasis, Variceal Management, and Post-Procedure Care | `ugib_variceal` | Variceal Bleeding: Band Ligation, TIPS, Beta-Blockers, and Prophylaxis — ESOPHAGEAL VARICEAL BLEEDING: ACUTE MANAGEMENT: HEMOSTASIS ENDOSCOPY (within 12h): BAND LIGATION (EVL: endoscopic variceal ligation; banding device; first-line; superior to sclerotherapy; repeat every 2-4 weeks until eradication; fewer sessions needed); SCLEROTHERAPY (sodium tetradecyl sulfate; ethanolamine; polidocanol; injection into varix; more complications than EVL: mediastinitis; ulceration; stricture; use if ligation not possible); SENGSTAKEN-BLAKEMORE TUBE (esophageal balloon tamponade: SALVAGE bridge if massive uncontrolled variceal bleed and TIPS not immediately available; NOT first-line; complication: aspiration; esophageal rupture; limit 12-24h then definitive therapy); SELF-EXPANDING METAL STENTS (SX-Ella Danis: removable esophageal stent; bridge to TIPS; avoids risks of balloon tamponade; effective hemorrhage control without compromising endoscopy); TIPS (Transjugular Intrahepatic Portosystemic Shunt): EARLY TIPS (within 72h; especially within 24h for high-risk [Child-Pugh C or B + active bleeding at endoscopy]): PREEMPTIVE TIPS (GARCIA-PAGAN NEJM 2010: early TIPS vs pharmacological + endoscopic in high-risk variceal bleeding: actuarial probability rebleeding at 1 year 3% vs 50%); TIPS INDICATION: failed endoscopic + pharmacological therapy; refractory variceal bleed; prevention rebleed in high-risk (Child-Pugh C); TIPS TECHNIQUE: IR procedure; 8-12 mm polytetrafluoroethylene (PTFE-covered) stent from hepatic vein to portal vein; reduces portal pressure below 12 mmHg; echocardiogram before (TR; RV function: cardio-pulmonary reserve); CONTRAINDICATIONS: hepatic encephalopathy PRIOR to TIPS (worsens HE); primary biliary cholangitis; polycystic liver; severe hepatic failure; congestive heart failure; COMPLICATIONS: hepatic encephalopathy (increased ammonia delivery to systemic circulation: lactulose + rifaximin; adjust stent diameter if refractory HE); hepatic failure; stent occlusion (use covered stents: longer patency); SECONDARY PROPHYLAXIS (PREVENT REBLEED POST-ACUTE): NON-SELECTIVE BETA-BLOCKERS + EVL: NADOLOL or PROPRANOLOL (reduce portal pressure; reduce cardiac output + splanchnic vasoconstriction; initiate once hemostasis achieved; target HR 55-60 bpm); CARVEDILOL (ALPHA1 + NON-SELECTIVE BETA: PREDESCI trial: superior to EVL for high-risk varices primary prophylaxis; FDA for secondary prophylaxis); COMBINATION EVL + NSBB: BOTH required for secondary prophylaxis (reduce rebleed 30-40% vs each alone); PRIMARY PROPHYLAXIS (FIRST VARICEAL BLEED PREVENTION): BAVENO VII GUIDELINES: SMALL VARICES + LOW-RISK: NSBB if high-risk features (red signs; Child-Pugh C); MEDIUM-LARGE VARICES: NSBB preferred (propranolol 20-40 mg twice daily; nadolol 20-40 mg/day) OR EVL (EVL + NSBB if NSBB intolerant); HEPATIC VENOUS PRESSURE GRADIENT (HVPG): target below 12 mmHg = no variceal bleed risk; below 20% reduction from baseline = respond; GASTRIC VARICES: TIPS or BRTO (balloon-occluded retrograde transvenous obliteration: fills with sclerosant; favors fundal varices type 2; access via gastrorenal shunt) | `text` |  |  |  |

PML-RARA; t(15;17); MEDICAL EMERGENCY): DIFFERENTIATION SYNDROME (DS; formerly retinoic acid syndrome): LIFE-THREATENING; occurs with ATRA + ATO during differentiation of APL blasts: SYMPTOMS: respiratory distress; fever; weight gain; peripheral edema; pulmonary infiltrates; renal failure; hypotension; pericardial effusion; MANAGEMENT: HIGH-DOSE DEXAMETHASONE 10 mg IV Q12h AT FIRST SIGN OF DS (do not wait for full syndrome); HOLD ATRA/ATO if severe (reintroduce when resolved); DIC MANAGEMENT (present in most APL at diagnosis): cryoprecipitate (maintain fibrinogen above 150 mg/dL); platelet transfusion (maintain PLT above 30-50k; especially during high bleeding risk); FFP (maintain PT/PTT near normal); ANTI-ATRA + ATO TREATMENT: LOW-INTERMEDIATE RISK (WBC below 10k): ATRA 45 mg/m2/day PO divided twice daily + ARSENIC TRIOXIDE (ATO) 0.15 mg/kg IV daily (APL0406 trial; Iland et al; NEJM 2013; CHEMOTHERAPY-FREE regimen for low-intermediate risk APL: 2-year EFS 97% vs 86% ATRA + anthracycline; FDA 2000 ATO relapsed/refractory; FDA extension 2018 newly diagnosed); HIGH-RISK APL (WBC above 10k): ATRA + ATO + ANTHRACYCLINE (idarubicin or cytarabine-containing; GEMTUZUMAB ozogamicin added in some protocols; Lo-Coco NEJM 2013 confirmed ATRA+ATO for low-intermediate; high-risk patients need anthracycline addition in most guidelines); CONSOLIDATION: ATRA + ATO x4 cycles (28-day cycles); MAINTENANCE: controversial (some protocols omit if PCR-negative after consolidation); PCR MONITORING: PML-RARA by RT-PCR Q3 months during consolidation + maintenance + 2 years after; MOLECULAR REMISSION = undetectable PML-RARA; RELAPSE: ATO alone (60-70% CR2); arsenic-ATRA combination; HSCT (allo or auto in CR2 based on PCR status); gemtuzumab (MYLOTARG); QTc monitoring (ATRA + ATO both prolong QTc; baseline ECG; electrolyte correction; Mg; K; hold if QTc above 500 ms; ophthalmology follow-up for ATRA pseudotumor cerebri); APL COAGULOPATHY (ALWAYS treat emergently; do not delay ATRA for lab confirmation): initiate ATRA immediately at APL diagnosis by morphology PENDING cytogenetics/FISH | `text` |  |  |  |

ESCALATE to carbapenem/vancomycin/micafungin if persistent; G-CSF not routine during induction); transfusion thresholds (RBC HgB above 7-8; PLT above 10k [20k if fever]; bleeding risk PLT above 50k); tumor lysis prophylaxis (allopurinol; rasburicase high-risk); PROPHYLAXIS: fluconazole (or posaconazole if high-risk neutropenia above 7 days; NCCN preferred antifungal); acyclovir (HSV); PNEUMOCYSTIS (TMP-SMX after reconstitution post-HCT); BIMODAL AGE: children <5 years + adults 40-60 years; FEMALE PREDOMINANCE (adults); SPORADIC (80%) vs. HEREDITARY (20%): LI-FRAUMENI SYNDROME (TP53 germline; most common hereditary); BECKWITH-WIEDEMANN (IGF2 overexpression); MEN-1 (MENIN); CARNEY COMPLEX; PRESENTATION: INCIDENTALOMA (30-40%): found on imaging for other reasons; FUNCTIONAL TUMOR (60%): hormone excess; MASS EFFECT: abdominal pain; early satiety; HORMONE EXCESS PRESENTATIONS: HYPERCORTISOLISM (Cushing syndrome; most common): moon facies; central obesity; striae; myopathy; osteoporosis; diabetes; HYPERANDROGENISM: VIRILIZATION (females): hirsutism; acne; clitoromegaly; menstrual irregularity; rapid virilization suggests ACC (more gradual in PCOD); FEMINIZATION (males): gynecomastia; impotence; testicular atrophy (rare; ESTROGEN-secreting); HYPERALDOSTERONISM: hypertension; hypokalemia (ALDO-secreting); MIXED (common in ACC): multiple hormone excess simultaneously; PRECLINICAL HYPERCORTISOLISM: autonomous cortisol secretion without Cushing features; BIOCHEMICAL EVALUATION (ALL ADRENAL MASSES): CORTISOL AUTONOMY: OVERNIGHT DST (1 mg dexamethasone); post-DST cortisol >=1.8 mcg/dL = autonomous; >=5.0 = likely Cushing; ANDROGENS: DHEA-S (elevated in ACC); TESTOSTERONE (females); ANDROSTENEDIONE; 17-HYDROXYPROGESTERONE; ALDOSTERONISM: ALDOSTERONE/RENIN RATIO if hypertension or hypokalemia; ESTROGEN (if feminizing in males); IMAGING: CT ADRENAL PROTOCOL: UNENHANCED HU: <10 HU = lipid-rich adenoma (benign); >10 HU suspicious; CONTRAST WASHOUT: >60% absolute; >40% relative = adenoma; ACC: LARGE (most >4 cm; many >6 cm; heterogeneous with necrosis/hemorrhage); IRREGULAR; INVASION; CALCIFICATION; PET/CT: staging; WEISS CRITERIA (HISTOLOGIC): MALIGNANCY: >=3 OF 9 CRITERIA: 1) HIGH NUCLEAR GRADE (Fuhrman 3-4); 2) MITOTIC RATE >5 per 50 HPF; 3) ATYPICAL MITOSES; 4) CLEAR CELLS <25% of tumor; 5) DIFFUSE ARCHITECTURE (>33% of tumor); 6) MICROSCOPIC NECROSIS; 7) VENOUS INVASION; 8) SINUSOIDAL INVASION; 9) CAPSULAR INVASION; KI-67 PROLIFERATION INDEX: important prognostic marker; HIGH Ki-67 (>10-20%) = poor prognosis | `text` |  |  |  |

LiMAX test); PORTAL HYPERTENSION (relative contraindication: HVPG above 10 mmHg; platelet below 100k; splenomegaly; varices; associated with poor outcomes; assess: liver stiffness; upper GI endoscopy); MARGINS (wide resection if possible; anatomic segmentectomy; LR resection equally curative if adequate margin); LIVER TRANSPLANTATION (LT; CURATIVE; REMOVES TUMOR + UNDERLYING CIRRHOSIS; Milan criteria for STANDARD listing): MILAN CRITERIA: 1 nodule below 5 cm OR up to 3 nodules each below 3 cm; NO MACROVASCULAR INVASION; NO EHS; 5-year OS above 70% WITHIN Milan; UCSF CRITERIA (EXPANDED; 1 nodule below 6.5 cm OR up to 3 nodules all below 4.5 cm + sum diameter below 8 cm; 5-year OS 75%); DOWNSTAGING TO MILAN: systemic or locoregional therapy to reduce tumor burden to within Milan criteria; maintained 3-6 months confirming stability; LT acceptable; NET ALLOCATION SCORE (NAS; US): AUTOMATIC MELD EXCEPTION POINTS for HCC within Milan criteria; 28-day delay before exception points; staged progression; BRIDGE THERAPY (TO PREVENT DROPOUT ON WAITLIST: TACE; TARE; RFA; SBRT; systemic; goal: maintain within Milan while awaiting donor; prevent waitlist dropout above 10% threshold); RADIOFREQUENCY ABLATION (RFA; percutaneous; heat; 3-5 cm zone coagulation; IMAGE-GUIDED [US; CT; MRI]; local recurrence below 5 cm nodule: 15-20% at 5 years; single-needle or multi-needle; adequate margin [5 mm tumor-free margin]; contraindications: near portal vein [heat sink]; near biliary tree; coagulopathy; risk: tumor seeding [uncommon with coaxial technique]); MICROWAVE ABLATION (MWA; larger ablation zone; faster; less heat sink effect vs RFA; REPLACING RFA at many centers; 2-6 cm effective; single session larger volumes; preferred near vessels; pacemaker [no electrical current vs RF]); ETHANOL INJECTION (PEI; old technique; largely replaced by RFA; still used for small lesions near biliary tree or vessels); STEREOTACTIC BODY RADIOTHERAPY (SBRT; locoregional; EXTERNAL BEAM; 3-5 fractions 40-60 Gy; excellent local control; used when RFA technically not feasible [location; size]; bridge to LT; CLEAN [SBRT] vs TACE debate]; emerging for HCC with PVT); IRREVERSIBLE ELECTROPORATION (IRE; NanoKnife; non-thermal; for tumors near bile duct or blood vessels; limited data; specialized) | `text` |  |  |  |

| Hepatocellular Carcinoma: Surveillance, Diagnosis, Staging, and Treatment | HCC Locoregional Therapy and Systemic Therapy by Line | `hcc_systemic_tx` | Advanced HCC Systemic Therapy: IMbrave150, HIMALAYA, Second Line, and Sorafenib Historical -- ADVANCED HCC SYSTEMIC THERAPY (BCLC C; MACROVASCULAR INVASION or EHS; OR TACE-REFRACTORY BCLC B): FIRST-LINE (PREFERRED): ATEZOLIZUMAB + BEVACIZUMAB (atezo/bev; anti-PDL1 + anti-VEGF; IMbrave150 NEJM 2020: atezo + bev vs sorafenib in BCLC C or TACE-refractory HCC [Child-Pugh A; ECOG 0-1; no varices or treated varices; anticoagulation NOT for PVT in this trial]: OS 19.2 vs 13.4 months; HR 0.66; PFS 6.8 vs 4.3 months; ORR 27.3% vs 11.9%; FDA 2020 first-line advanced HCC): PRE-SCREENING REQUIRED: EGD WITHIN 6 MONTHS (rule out high-risk varices; EVL banding if needed before starting bev; bevacizumab variceal bleeding risk); NO BEVACIZUMAB if active bleeding; recent hemoptysis; uncontrolled HTN; THROMBOEMBOLISM; SECOND PREFERRED FIRST-LINE: TREMELIMUMAB + DURVALUMAB (STRIDE regimen: tremelimumab [anti-CTLA4] single priming dose 300 mg IV + durvalumab [anti-PDL1] 1500 mg Q4W; HIMALAYA TRIAL JCO 2022: STRIDE vs sorafenib vs durvalumab alone: STRIDE OS 16.4 vs 13.8 months sorafenib; HR 0.78; p=0.0035; FDA 2022 first-line advanced HCC; SINGLE DOSE TREMELIMUMAB avoids cumulative CTLA4 toxicity): ADVANTAGES vs atezo/bev: NO BEVACIZUMAB (safer in variceal bleeding; portal vein thrombosis; no EGD required; thrombosis risk); PREFERENCE: atezo/bev if varices controlled + no bevacizumab contraindications; STRIDE if bevacizumab contraindicated; HISTORICAL FIRST-LINE: SORAFENIB (Nexavar; multi-kinase inhibitor; VEGFR/PDGFR/RAF; FDA 2007 first-line HCC; SHARP trial NEJM 2008: sorafenib vs placebo: OS 10.7 vs 7.9 months; first systemic therapy proven benefit HCC; standard 2007-2020; 400 mg PO twice daily; AEs: hand-foot skin reaction [HFSR; grade 3 in 8%; dose reduction]; diarrhea; HTN; fatigue; QTc; thyroid; DRUG INTERACTIONS CYP3A4/2C9); LENVATINIB (Lenvima; VEGFR1-3; FGFR1-4; PDGFR-alpha; KIT; RET; FDA 2018 first-line; REFLECT trial NEJM 2018: lenvatinib non-inferior to sorafenib [OS 13.6 vs 12.3 months]; better ORR [24.1% vs 9.2%]; better PFS [7.4 vs 3.7 months]; once daily 8-12 mg PO; dose based on weight; PREFERRED by some over sorafenib; AEs: HTN [53%; common]; proteinuria; fatigue; diarrhea; HFSR less than sorafenib; QALY benefit; SECOND-LINE AFTER ANTI-VEGF: RAMUCIRUMAB (anti-VEGFR2; REACH-2: AFP above 400 ng/mL; 8 mg/kg IV Q2W vs placebo: OS 8.5 vs 7.3 months; ONLY second-line agent proven in AFP-HIGH subgroup; FDA 2019 AFP above 400); CABOZANTINIB (CELESTIAL NEJM 2018: OS 10.2 vs 8.0 months; VEGFR; MET; AXL; FDA 2019 2L HCC; AEs: HFSR; HTN; diarrhea; GI perforation); REGORAFENIB (RESORCE NEJM 2017: prior sorafenib progression; OS 10.6 vs 7.8 months; FDA 2017 2L HCC; sequence sorafenib THEN regorafenib [similar mechanism]); NIVOLUMAB (SECOND LINE; CheckMate-040: FDA 2017 accelerated approval; CheckMate-9DW: nivo + ipi vs sorafenib or lenvatinib FIRST LINE: OS 23.1 vs 16.0 months; HR 0.79; FDA 2024 first line nivo + ipilimumab); PEMBROLIZUMAB (KEYNOTE-240: 2L post-sorafenib; OS benefit 13.9 vs 10.6 months in analysis; FDA 2018 accelerated approval; KEYNOTE-224; confirmatory trial; 2L post-sorafenib or post-1L ICI); ANTI-GPC3 (glypican-3; HCC-targeted; T-cell redirecting bispecifics + CAR-T research); biomarkers: GPC3 expression; WNT/beta-catenin mutation (predicts ICI resistance; APC/CTNNB1-mutated HCC less responsive to ICI); AFP; DCP; TMB generally low in HCC | `text` |  |  |  |

| Pancreatic Adenocarcinoma: Staging, Resectability, and Systemic Therapy | PDAC Diagnosis, Staging, Resectability Classification, and Molecular Profiling | `pdac_resect` | PDAC Resectability Criteria, Whipple Procedure, and Adjuvant Chemotherapy — RESECTABILITY CLASSIFICATION (NCCN/AHPBA/SSO/SSAT CONSENSUS): RESECTABLE (R0 goal): NO ARTERIAL CONTACT (SMA; CA; CHA); VENOUS: PV-SMV contact below 180 degrees WITHOUT contour deformity OR above 180 degrees WITHOUT vein irregularity; NO DISTANT METASTASES; BORDERLINE RESECTABLE (BLR): VENOUS: above 180 degree PV-SMV contact or reconstructable occlusion; SHORT SEGMENT narrowing or distortion PV-SMV; ARTERIAL: SOLID CONTACT SMA or CA up to 180 degrees; SOLID CONTACT COMMON HEPATIC ARTERY (CHA) with short segment; ABERRANT ANATOMY (accessory right hepatic artery; replaced RHA; reconstructable); LOCALLY ADVANCED (LA; unresectable): SOLID CONTACT SMA or CA above 180 degrees; AORTIC CONTACT; UNRECONSTRUCTABLE VENOUS OCCLUSION; METASTATIC: peritoneal or distant organ metastases; SURGICAL PROCEDURES: WHIPPLE PROCEDURE (PANCREATICODUODENECTOMY; standard for pancreatic head/uncinate; classic: resects distal stomach; pylorus-preserving [PPPD: preserves pylorus + proximal 3-4 cm duodenum; equally curative; less delayed gastric emptying]; reconstruction: hepaticojejunostomy; pancreaticojejunostomy or pancreaticogastrostomy; gastrojejunostomy; PANCREATIC FISTULA [POPF: grade A/B/C; main postoperative complication 10-25%; RiskScore based on duct size + texture]); DISTAL PANCREATECTOMY (pancreatic body/tail; includes splenectomy for en-bloc resection; minimally invasive: MIPS equivalent to open DIPLOMA trial); TOTAL PANCREATECTOMY (extent resection; eliminates POPF risk; brittle diabetes inevitable; autoimmune islet transplant option); ADJUVANT CHEMOTHERAPY (AFTER RESECTION; 3-6 months): FOLFIRINOX x6 months: MODIFIED mFOLFIRINOX (PRODIGE-24/CCTG PA.6 NEJM 2018: mFOLFIRINOX [oxaliplatin 85 mg/m2; leucovorin 400 mg/m2; irinotecan 150 mg/m2 [reduced from 180 mg/m2 original]; fluorouracil 2400 mg/m2 over 46h; Q14 days] vs gemcitabine; DFS 21.4 months vs 12.8 months; OS 54.4 months vs 35 months; 3-year OS 63.4% vs 48.6%; STANDARD OF CARE for fit PDAC adjuvant [ECOG 0-1; no peripheral neuropathy above grade 1; adequate biliary decompression]); GEM/CAPECITABINE (ESPAC-4 NEJM 2017: gemcitabine + capecitabine vs gemcitabine alone: OS 28.0 vs 25.5 months; FDA approved; for patients not fit for FOLFIRINOX); SINGLE-AGENT GEMCITABINE (Burris NEJM 1997 landmark; adjuvant CONKO-001 NEJM 2007; now second-line adjuvant to FOLFIRINOX or gem/cap); ADJUVANT RT + CHEMORADIOTHERAPY: NOT ROUTINELY RECOMMENDED (ESPAC-1; RTOG 9704; studies conflicting; consider for R1 margins or node-positive disease at select centers; ACS expert opinion); CHEMORADIOTHERAPY FOR R1 (positive margins): 5-FU or gemcitabine-based CRT followed by systemic treatment; no OS benefit proven vs systemic alone; NCCN supports CRT for borderline/locally advanced downstaging | `text` |  |  |  |

| Pancreatic Adenocarcinoma: Staging, Resectability, and Systemic Therapy | Metastatic and Locally Advanced PDAC: Systemic Therapy and Maintenance | `pdac_la` | Locally Advanced PDAC: Neoadjuvant Therapy, Conversion Surgery, and Palliative Care — LOCALLY ADVANCED PDAC (LA-PDAC) MANAGEMENT: SYSTEMIC CHEMOTHERAPY FIRST (FOLFIRINOX or nab-pac/gem x4-6 months: downstaging; exclude early progressors; CA 19-9 decline + CT response; radiographic criteria NOT always predict resectability); RESTAGING CT Q2-3 cycles; CONVERSION SURGERY (minority; 10-15% of LA-PDAC become resectable; exploration recommended if: CA19-9 normalization + CT stable or improving; consider resection attempt after 6+ months FOLFIRINOX); CHEMORADIOTHERAPY (CRT): ROLE DEBATED IN LA-PDAC; LAP-07 trial (randomized CRT vs observation after 4 months gem): no OS benefit CRT; ALLIANCE A021501 TRIAL (ECOG/ACRIN 2020: borderline resectable: chemotherapy alone vs mFOLFIRINOX + SBRT [stereotactic body radiotherapy]: SBRT arm CLOSED early for FUTILITY [worse outcomes]); NCCN RECOMMENDATION: CHEMORADIATION after chemotherapy induction for LA-PDAC IF tumor remains unresectable after 4-6 cycles; BORDERLINE RESECTABLE: upfront FOLFIRINOX/nab-pac x3-6 cycles then CRT 50.4 Gy with 5-FU or capecitabine-based; restaging then surgery if no PD; PANCREATIC STEREOTACTIC BODY RADIOTHERAPY (SBRT; 5 fractions 33-40 Gy or 3 fractions; faster delivery; ABLATIVE DOSE; PRECISION; hepatic tolerance limit 20 Gy/L; duodenum constraints; promising for pain + local control; improving local failure rates); BILIARY DRAINAGE (PREOPERATIVE OR PALLIATIVE): ERCP + BILIARY STENT (plastic 3-4 months; METAL [FCSEMS] preferred for unresectable; longer patency 10-12 months vs 3-4 months plastic); EUS-HEPATICOGASTROSTOMY (alternative if ERCP fails; papillary obstruction; prior surgical anatomy); PERCUTANEOUS TRANSHEPATIC BILIARY DRAINAGE (PTBD; when above fail); DUODENAL OBSTRUCTION (gastric outlet obstruction in advanced PDAC): DUODENAL STENT (endoscopic self-expanding metal stent; PALLIATIVE first-line); LAPAROSCOPIC GASTROJEJUNOSTOMY (durable surgical bypass; preferred if life expectancy above 3-6 months; can combine with CJ at time of exploration); PAIN MANAGEMENT: NEUROLYTIC CELIAC PLEXUS BLOCK (NCPB; EUS-guided; reduces opioid requirement; ORR 80% for visceral pain; repeat if needed; PCPB percutaneous); OPIOID ROTATION; SUSTAINED-RELEASE OPIOIDS; ADJUNCTIVE: pregabalin; gabapentin; TCAD; KETAMINE infusion refractory pain; EARLY PALLIATIVE CARE INTEGRATION (QOL; functional goals; caregiver support; ADVANCE CARE PLANNING); NUTRITIONAL SUPPORT: PANCREATIC EXOCRINE INSUFFICIENCY (PEI; 80% PDAC at diagnosis): PANCREATIC ENZYME REPLACEMENT THERAPY (PERT; lipase 40,000-80,000 IU per meal; 20,000-40,000 IU per snack; take WITH FOOD; not before/after; CREON; Zenpep; titrate to symptoms); ELEMENTAL FORMULA; MEDIUM-CHAIN TRIGLYCERIDES (MCT); VITAMIN SUPPLEMENTATION (fat-soluble; B12); GLUCOSE CONTROL (new-onset DM from PDAC itself: metformin; insulin; avoid sulfonylureas [hypoglycemia risk in eating-impaired]); OLAPARIB POST-PLATINUM MAINTENANCE (POLO criteria: germline BRCA1/2 + mPDAC + no PD after 16 weeks platinum-containing regimen; 300 mg BID; PFS benefit 7.4 vs 3.8 months; NCCN preferred; OS benefit not statistically significant but prolonged PFS meaningful in select BRCA-mutated patients) | `text` |  |  |  |

EFS IMPROVED; FDA 2022 NIVOLUMAB NEOADJUVANT RESECTABLE NSCLC IB-IIIA]; PEMBROLIZUMAB NEOADJUVANT [KEYNOTE-671: NEOADJUVANT CARBO+TAXOL + PEMBRO x4 THEN SURGERY + PEMBRO ADJUVANT x13 CYCLES: pCR 18.1% vs 4%; EFS IMPROVED; FDA 2023]; PERIOPERATIVE OSIMERTINIB [IF EGFR MUTATION: ADJUVANT OSIMERTINIB ADAURA; NEOADJUVANT EGFR DATA EMERGING]; INDUCTION CHEMORADIATION [SOME CENTERS; DEBATE]; UNRESECTABLE STAGE III [MOST STAGE III; CONCURRENT CHEMORADIATION STANDARD]: CONCURRENT CHEMORADIATION [CARBOPLATIN AUC 2 QW + PACLITAXEL 45-50 mg/m2 QW + RT 60-66 GY IN 30-33 FRACTIONS; OR CISPLATIN 50 mg/m2 DAY 1/8 + ETOPOSIDE 50 mg/m2 DAYS 1-5 Q4W x2 CONCURRENT + 60-66 GY; KEY PRINCIPLE: CONCURRENT > SEQUENTIAL [CHEMOTHERAPY + RADIATION SIMULTANEOUSLY; RADIOSENSITIZATION]; INDUCTION THEN CONCURRENT ALSO USED; RADIATION PLANNING [4D-CT; PET-CT FUSION; IGRT; IMRT; PROTON BEAM THERAPY AT SELECT CENTERS]; ORGAN AT RISK CONSTRAINTS [MEAN LUNG DOSE BELOW 20 GY; V20 BELOW 35%; HEART CONSTRAINTS; SPINAL CORD BELOW 45 GY]; ESOPHAGITIS MANAGEMENT [GRADE 2-3 COMMON; VISCOUS LIDOCAINE + ANTACID MIXTURE; SUCRALFATE; NUTRITION SUPPORT; DOSE MODIFICATION IF GRADE 3]; RADIATION PNEUMONITIS [1-3 MONTHS POST RT; DYSPNEA + COUGH + FEVER; CONSOLIDATION; PREDNISONE 1 mg/kg TAPER x6-8 WEEKS; MONITOR PFTs]]; PACIFIC REGIMEN [DURVALUMAB MAINTENANCE; LANDMARK]: PACIFIC TRIAL NEJM 2017 [UNRESECTABLE STAGE III NSCLC NOT PROGRESSING AFTER CONCURRENT CHEMORADIATION: DURVALUMAB 10 mg/kg IV Q2W x12 MONTHS vs PLACEBO: PFS 16.8 vs 5.6 MONTHS; OS 47.5 vs 29.1 MONTHS; 4-YEAR OS 49.6% vs 36.3%; FDA 2018 STANDARD OF CARE CONSOLIDATION]; ELIGIBILITY [NO PROGRESSION AFTER CHEMORADIATION; START WITHIN 42 DAYS; PD-L1 ABOVE 1% INITIALLY REQUIRED; FDA LATER REMOVED PDL1 REQUIREMENT]; ADVERSE EFFECTS [IMMUNE-RELATED PNEUMONITIS [5-7% GRADE 3-4; OVERLAP WITH RT PNEUMONITIS; MONITOR]; IMMUNOTHERAPY TOXICITIES [COLITIS; HEPATITIS; ENDOCRINOPATHIES]]; EGFR/ALK IN STAGE III [MOLECULAR TESTING STILL RECOMMENDED]: EGFR MUTATION [ADJUVANT OSIMERTINIB ADAURA; IF RESECTED STAGE IB-IIIA WITH EGFR MUTATION: 3 YEARS OSIMERTINIB]; ALK/ROS1 REARRANGEMENT [LESS DATA FOR ADJUVANT; IF TREATED WITH CHEMORADIATION: DURVALUMAB PACIFIC DATA INCLUDED; ALECTINIB ALINA TRIAL: ADJUVANT ALECTINIB IN ALK+ EARLY-STAGE]; PDL1 TESTING FOR PACIFIC [TUMOR PROPORTION SCORE] | `text` |  |  |  |

NNT 2.8; DEFUSE-3 NEJM 2018: EVT 6-16h with CTP mismatch (core below 70 mL + mismatch ratio above 1.8): mRS 0-2 44.6% vs 16.7%; BOTH trials stopped early for overwhelming efficacy; AHA 2018 CLASS I RECOMMENDATION for EVT up to 24h in selected patients with clinical-imaging mismatch (NIHSS above 10 + core below 70 mL + mismatch ratio above 1.8); POSTERIOR CIRCULATION LVO (BASILAR ARTERY OCCLUSION; BAO): HIGH MORTALITY untreated; BASICS TRIAL 2021: EVT vs best medical treatment in BAO: no significant difference (wide confidence intervals; few patients); BEST TRIAL 2020 (China): EVT vs conservative in BAO: EVT trended better; CURRENT PRACTICE: EVT for basilar artery occlusion generally accepted at most centers; individualized; BASILAR mRS 0-2 very low even with EVT (above 30-40%); TECHNIQUE: STENT RETRIEVERS (Solitaire; Trevo; Revive; frontline); CONTACT ASPIRATION (ADAPT technique; direct aspiration catheter; Penumbra ACE; ASTER trial: aspiration = stent retriever); COMBINED (balloon guide catheter + stent retriever + aspiration); | Autonomic Disorders — POTS, OH, MSA, Diabetic Autonomic Neuropathy | MSA and Diabetic Autonomic Neuropathy | `msa` | Multiple System Atrophy (MSA) and Autonomic Testing (MULTIPLE SYSTEM ATROPHY (MSA): ALPHA-SYNUCLEINOPATHY (like PD, DLB); ALPHA-SYNUCLEIN in glial cytoplasmic inclusions (not Lewy bodies); CEREBELLAR PREDOMINANT (MSA-C): ataxia, dysarthria, nystagmus; vs. PARKINSONIAN (MSA-P): parkinsonism, poor levodopa response; AUTONOMIC FAILURE: severe nOH, urinary incontinence, erectile dysfunction (early and severe); LARYNGEAL STRIDOR: inspiratory stridor during sleep (vocal cord abductor paralysis); OSA; DIAGNOSIS: 2022 MDS CRITERIA: clinically probable MSA = autonomic failure + cerebellar/parkinsonism + MRI changes; MRI: hot cross bun sign (pons T2); atrophy of pons/cerebellum/putamen; putaminal hypointensity; DATscan/FDG-PET-CT; PROGNOSIS: survival 6-10 years; rapid deterioration; AUTONOMIC TESTING BATTERY: QSART (quantitative sudomotor axon reflex test): postganglionic sudomotor; thermoregulatory sweat test (TST); cardiovagal testing (Valsalva, deep breathing HR response); VALSALVA RATIO: normal >=1.5; impaired in autonomic neuropathy; cardiovagal: beat-to-beat HR variation; ADRENERGIC: blood pressure response to Valsalva; TILT TABLE: HR/BP response; PURE AUTONOMIC FAILURE (PAF): isolated; no motor involvement; Lewy bodies; slowly progressive; DIABETIC AUTONOMIC NEUROPATHY (DAN): most common cause; CARDIAC AUTONOMIC NEUROPATHY: elevated resting HR; reduced HRV; QTc prolongation; silent MI; increased CV mortality; GASTROINTESTINAL: gastroparesis; constipation; diarrhea (osmotic, exocrine); BLADDER: neurogenic; incomplete emptying; ERECTILE DYSFUNCTION: often first symptom in men; SWEATING: anhidrosis; hyperhidrosis) | `text` |  |  |  |

MAINTENANCE: 1 g/kg Q3-4 weeks OR 0.4 g/kg Q2-3 weeks; ICE STUDY: IVIG superior to placebo in preventing relapse; PATH STUDY: SCIG (subcutaneous Ig) 0.2 g/kg/week (Hizentra) maintains remission after IVIG; MEASURE FUNCTIONAL RESPONSE at 12 weeks; TAPER to lowest effective dose; HYQVIA (facilitated SCIg): 10% hyaluronidase + IVIG Q4W; larger volume SC infusion; 2) CORTICOSTEROIDS: PREDNISONE: 1 mg/kg/day (max 80 mg) x4-6 weeks, then gradual taper; or PULSE DEXAMETHASONE: 40 mg PO days 1-4 monthly x6 cycles (PREDICT study); TYPICAL CIDP responds to steroids; PURE MOTOR CIDP: steroids may WORSEN (avoid in MMN by mistake); SIDE EFFECTS: bone loss; diabetes; hypertension; mood; 3) PLASMAPHERESIS/PLASMA EXCHANGE (PLEX): 5-6 exchanges over 2 weeks; short-lived (requires frequent maintenance); useful for RAPID RESPONSE need (severe CIDP, acute onset);
